L1550: Immunogenicity was assessed in this study. In the 240 \mu g/kg cohort, one subject had a positive binding assay at baseline; however, the confirmatory assay was not positive. In the 320 \mu g/kg cohort, one subject had a positive binding assay at baseline and one subject had a positive binding assay on Day 13 of cycle 2; however, the confirmatory assays for both cases were not positive.
L1551: **Reviewer's Comments:** The C_{max} and AUC_{0-t} increased with increasing dose in those receiving TAC chemotherapy. This is in contrast to those receiving EC chemotherapy where C_{max} did not increase between the 240 and 320 \mu g/kg doses in the first dosing cycle. The AUC_{0-t} and C_{max} were lower in cycle 3 compared to cycle 1.
L1552: *The 320 µg/kg dose appears to have a higher PD effect than the 240 µg/kg dose in those receiving TAC chemotherapy. The duration of grade 3 or 4 neutropenia, the duration of grade 4 neutropenia, the ANC nadir, and the number of days when ANC recovered to 1.0\times 10^9/L from nadir was more favorable in those dosed with 320 µg/kg in both the first cycle and overall throughout all 6 cycles of chemotherapy.*
L1553: ## **A Phase 3, Randomized, Multi-Centre, Open-Label, Fixed Dose, Neulasta® Active-Controlled Clinical Trial of F-627 in Women With Breast Cancer Receiving Myelotoxic Chemotherapy (Trial GC-627-05)**
L1554: ## **Study Design**
L1555: This phase 3 trial randomized female subjects with stage I to stage III breast cancer who were receiving neoadjuvant or adjuvant myelotoxic taxane + cyclophosphamide (TC; 75 mg/m2 docetaxel +600 mg/m2 cyclophosphamide) chemotherapy to receive either the study drug (20 mg PFS) or Neulasta (6 mg). Study participation was limited to the first 4 cycles of chemotherapy. The primary objective was to evaluate the efficacy of efbemalenograstim alfa-vuxw compared to Neulasta in the first chemotherapy cycle. The safety objective was to assess safety in subjects treated with efbemalenograstim alfa-vuxw.
L1556: Those included in the study had a WBC count \ge 4.0\times 10^9/L, hemoglobin \ge 11.5 g/dL, platelet count \ge 150\times 10^9/L, and adequate renal, hepatic, and cardiac function. Sparse PK sampling was collected throughout cycles 1 and 3 of chemotherapy (Day 2 prior to efbemalenograstim alfa-vuxw or Neulasta administration, Day 4, and Day 8).
L1557: ## **Results**
L1558: Sparse PK samplings were taken in this study for popPK analysis in cycles 1 and 3. Section 14.5 discusses the popPK analysis results in greater detail. Table 117 summarizes the sparse PK samples collected during this study.

In chemotherapy cycle 1, both groups had 23 subjects (11.7%) that developed grade 4 neutropenia (Table 118). These numbers decreased with subsequent cycles. L1582: Immunogenicity was also assessed in this study, and results are presented in Table 121.
L1583: Thirty subjects treated with 20 mg efbemalenogastim alfa-vuxw had ADAs to the drug – 17 developed treatment-induced binding ADAs, 3 subjects tested positive at baseline only, and 10 subjects had binding ADAs at baseline and postbaseline, but not treatment boosted. In the efbemalenogastim alfa-vuxw group, no subjects developed neutralizing ADAs against efbemalenogastim alfa-vuxw, and no subjects developed treatment-boosted binding ADAs.
L1584: Eighteen subjects treated with 20 mg efbemalenogastim alfa-vuxw developed G-CSF ADAs – 15 developed treatment-emergent binding ADAs, 3 subjects tested positive at baseline only, no subjects had binding ADAs positive at baseline and postbaseline but not treatment-boosted, and no subjects had neutralizing ADAs against G-CSF. There were no subjects in the study that tested positive for treatment-emergent ADAs to both efbemalenogastim alfa-vuxw and G-CSF, there were no subjects who were positive for binding ADAs to efbemalenogastim alfa-vuxw and treatment emergent ADAs to G-CSF, and there were no subjects who were positive for binding ADAs to G-CSF and treatment emergent ADAs to efbemalenogastim alfa-vuxw.
L1585: Fifteen subjects treated with 6 mg Neulasta had ADAs to efbemalenogastim alfa-vuxw – 6 developed treatment-induced binding ADAs, 2 subjects tested positive at baseline only, and 7 subjects had binding ADAs at baseline and postbaseline, but not treatment boosted. There were 2 subjects treated with Neulasta that developed neutralizing ADAs against efbemalenogastim alfa-

L1601: ## 14.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L1602: The PK characteristics of efbemalenogastim alfa-vuxw were evaluated with ELISA quantification in serum samples. Development, quantification, and validation of the PK bioanalytical method and analyses of clinical samples were performed
L1603: The standard concentrations used in the analytical runs were 1.56, 3.125, 6.25, 12.5, 25.0, 50.0, and 100 ng/mL in human serum. The LLOQ was 1.56 ng/mL and the ULOQ was 100 ng/mL. Concentrations for QC samples in the analytical runs were 5.0, 20.0, and 75.0 ng/mL in human serum. When testing clinical samples, subject serum samples were diluted 1:10 immediately prior to assay. Bioanalytical reports below meet the prespecified criteria, and deviations are noted as appropriate.

L1609: - Calibration standard nominal concentrations:156.00, 312.00, 625.00, 1250.00, 2500.00, 5000.00, and 10000.00 pg/mL
- Quality control/validation samples:To have cross-validation with the client method, 2 sets of validation samples will be tested in accuracy and precision runs
- Nominal concentrations in 100% human serum: 468.00 (lower limit of quantification (LLOQ)), 1500.00 (low quality control (LoQC)), 6000.00 (medium quality control (MeQC)), 22500.00 (high quality control (HiQC)) and 30000.00 (upper limit of quantification (ULOQ)) pg/mLThese validation samples will be diluted 1:3 in calibrator diluent RD6A prior to analysis
- Nominal concentrations in 100% human serum: 1560.00 (LLOQ), 5000.00 (LoQC), 20000.00 (MeQC), 75000.00 (HiQC) and 100000.00 (ULOQ) pg/mLThese validation samples will be diluted 1:10 in calibrator diluent RD6A prior to analysis
- Samples will be analyzed in duplicate, and the mean of the duplicate results will be reportedPrecision between sample duplicates should not exceed 20% based upon back-calculated data
- Calibration CurveCalibration standards in the quantifiable range of 156.00 pg/mL to 10000.00 pg/mL in Calibrator Diluent RD6A must be within \pm 20\% relative error (RE) of the nominal concentration (\pm 25\% at the LLOQ and ULOQ) and have a precision (coefficient of variation percent (CV%)) between duplicates of \le 20\% (\le 25\% at the LLOQ and ULOQ)
- Six of seven standards within the quantifiable range of 156.00 pg/mL to 10000.00 pg/mL must be included, one of seven standards may be dropped or edited from the curve
- Intra-assay and interassay accuracy and precisionEach validation sample (LLOQ, LoQC, MeQC, HiQC and ULOQ) will be assayed in triplicate with a minimum of 3 batches performed on at least two days, by at least two analysis
- Target criteria:Precision \le 20\% (25% at LLOQ and ULOQ)
L1610: - Accuracy: within \pm 20\% relative error (RE) (25\% at LLOQ and ULOQ)
- Calibration Curve – Acceptance CriteriaPrecision &lt; 20\% (25\% at LLOQ and ULOQ)
- Accuracy: within +20\% relative error (RE) (25\% at LLOQ and ULOQ)
- QC Acceptance criteria:Two duplicates each for LoQC (5000.00 pg/mL), MeQC (20000.00 pg/mL), HiQC (75000.00 pg/mL) level will be assessed in each subsequent validation batch, and each QCs will be diluted 1 in 10 in Calibrator Diluent RD6A prior to analysisPrecision between sample replicates: \le 20\%
- 4 of the 6 QC samples analyzed must be \pm RE of the nominal value
- Interassay accuracy: within \pm 20\% RE of the nominal value
- Matrix effect:For the matrix effect, a minimum of 80% of the individual samples should be within \pm 20\% of their nominal values for each kind of matrix
- Dilution linearity:Dilution linearity is considered acceptable when the mean accuracy of the dilution samples with concentration between the LLOQ and ULOQ falls within \pm 20\% RE of the expected concentration with a precision of \le 20\%
- Matrix type: human serum
- Minimum required dilution: 1:3

L1612: - Matrix effect:Specificity and selectivity for normal human serum was assessed in VB05 and for disease matrix in VB06R
- All naïve individuals had results lower than the LLOQ of 468.00 pg/mL
- 10 normal human serum individuals were tested and 9 of 10 individual recoveries met the acceptance criteria of 80% to 120% accuracy, all 6 disease individual recoveries met the acceptance criteria
- The matrix effect data passed the acceptance criteria
- Room temperature stability:For LoQC and HiQC, recovery for samples at 6 hours room temperature was 107.4% and 94.9%, respectively, with precisions of 3.4% and 1.1%, respectively
- For LoQC and HiQC, recovery for samples at 24 hours room temperature was 104.1% and 87.0%, respectively, with precisions of 4.8% and 0.3%, respectively
- When stability QCs are compared to the baseline QCs, the % difference is 1.2% and -2.5% for the LoQC and HiQC subjected to 6 hours at room temperature, and -1.9% and 10.6% for the LoQC and HiQC subjected to 24 hours at room temperature
- Dilutional linearity:Acceptable recovery of the dilution range up to 1 in 2000 dilution (not taking the minimum required dilution of 1:3 into account) was observed with accuracy ranging between 94.1% and 96.7%
- Long-term freezer stability:The recovery for the LoQC, HiQC, and &gt; ULOQ samples that were subject to 181 days at -80°C to -60°C was 112.4%, 96.1%, and 95.2%, respectively, with precision values of 3.8%, 1.5%, and 4.2%, respectively
- For subsequent sample analysis, the following acceptance criteria should be applied:Accuracy 80-120% of the nominal concentration at 312.00 pg/mL to 5000.00 pg/mL, accuracy 75%-125% of the nominal concentration at 156.00 pg/mL and 10000.00 pg/mL
- Precision of \le 20\% at 312.00 pg/mL to 5000.00 pg/mL, \le 25\% at 156.00 pg/mL and 10000.00 pg/mL
- Minimum of 6 valid calibration points between 156.00 and 10000.00 pg/mL
- For QC samples, a minimum of 2/3, one at each level, are required to meet the above criteria for accuracy and precision of the batch to be considered valid
- Sample CriteriaPrecision of \le 20\% (does not apply to samples below the LLOQ)
- Samples should not be subject to more than 24 hours at room temperature
- Samples are stable up to 181 days at -80°C to -60°C
- No extrapolation above the ULOQ should occur
- No extrapolation below the LLOQ should occur
L1613: - The highest validated dilution for a sample is 1 in 2000 (in addition to MRD of 1:3)
- Method and Protocol Deviations:Minimum required dilution is 1:3, but the QC samples were diluted 1:10. To have a bridge with the 1:10 dilution method, 3 accuracy and precision runs with the 2 dilution factors were validated with 2 sets of validation samples. After the accuracy and precision runs, the QCs will continue with the dilution of 1:10 to be consistent with the client method in subsequent runs.
- In VB05, the QCs samples with concentration of 1500.00 (LoQC), 6000.00 (MeQC), 22500.00 (HiQC) were prepared and analyzed with MRD 1:3 instead of QCs samples of 5000.00 (LoQC), 20000.00 (MeQC), 75000.00 (HiQC) analyzed with the dilution of 1:10, deviating from the protocol.
- Because 2 sets of QCs with the dilution of 1:3 and 1:10 were validated and passed the criteria, the VB05 run was determined to be acceptable since the deviation has no impact on the study.

L1639: - There was linearity in the range of 156-10000 pg/mL based on the fitting of 4-parameter equation

- No extrapolation above the ULOQ or below the LLOQ should occur

## Ryzneuta (efbemalenograstim alfa-vuxw)

- A total of 15 runs were performed for sample analysis, including 1 qualification run. The 15 runs passed, and data were reported.

L1661: ## 14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L1662: ## 14.5.1. Summary of Applicant's Population PK Analysis
L1663: Population PK analysis was conducted using 2495 evaluable efbemalenogastim (F-627) concentrations in a total of 289 subjects, including 39 female subjects with breast cancer from three phase I trials 2012-F-627-CH1 (STDY 1), SP-CDR-1-1301 (STDY 2), SP11502 (STDY 3), 54 healthy male subjects from two phase I trials GC-F-627-01 (STDY 4), GC-F-627-03 (STDY 5), and 196 female subjects with breast cancer from the phase 3 trial GC-F-627-05 (STDY 6). The dose range included weight-based dosing (30, 60, 80, 120, 240, 320, and 360 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg}) and a 20 mg fixed dose. Summary statistics of the continuous and categorical covariates that were evaluated in the population PK analysis are shown in Table 123.

L1668: The Applicant claimed that a one-compartment model with first order absorption and elimination adequately described the efbemalenograstim concentration data. Lean body mass (LBM), age and dosing (weighted-adjusted dosing or fixed dosing) was found to influence CL/F; formulation was found to influence V/F; age and formulation were found to influence KA.
L1669: The final function form for population KA is expressed as below:
L1670: - For liquid formulation, TVKA = 0.0213 \times \exp(-0.0146 \times (\text{AGE}-47)) \times 1.427;
- For lyophilized formulation, TVKA = 0.0213 \times \exp(-0.0146 \times (\text{AGE}-47)).
L1671: The final function form for population CL/F is expressed as below:
L1672: - For fixed dosing, TVCL = 1.44 \times [(\text{LBM}/47.98) \times 0.803] \times [(\text{AGE}/47) \times 0.191] - 1.03;
- For weight-adjusted dosing, TVCL = 1.44 \times [(\text{LBM}/47.98) \times 0.803] \times [(\text{AGE}/47) \times 0.191].
L1673: The final population V/F is expressed as below:
L1674: - For liquid formulation, TVV = 7
- For lyophilized formulation, TVV = 20.3

L1687: **Reviewer's Comments:** The Applicant's final population PK model as described above is inadequate to describe the efbemalenograstim serum concentration-time profiles following the administration of efbemalenograstim ranged from 30 mg/kg to 360 mg/kg SC and 20 mg SC once every chemotherapy cycle. The dose escalation studies (GC-F-627-01 and 2012-F-627-CH1) indicated a saturable clearance mechanism for efbemalenograstim. The Applicant also claims in Section 12.3 of draft labeling that "serum clearance is directly related to the number of neutrophils." However, this saturable clearance mechanism was not implemented in Applicant's final PopPK model, which led to inaccurate prediction of the observed PK profiles. This was confirmed based on the Agency's independent Goodness-of-fit assessment (Figure 37) using the Applicant's final population PK model. The population prediction (PRED) versus observed (DV) (Figure 37 Panel A) and individual predictions (IPRED) versus DV (Figure 37 Panel B) plots indicate significant underpredictions of observed concentrations above 1000 ng/mL. The conditional weighted residuals (CWRES) versus PRED (Figure 37 Panel C) and CWRES versus Time (Figure 37 Panel D) also indicate significant trends of CWRES by PRED and over time. These GOF plots indicated that the Applicant's final PopPK model failed to well characterize the observed efbemalenograstim concentrations time profiles.
L1688: **Figure 37. Reviewer's Goodness-of-Fit Plots Stratified by Study and Treatment Cycle Based on Applicant's Final Population PK Model**
L1689: Source: Reviewer's analysis.
L1690: Abbreviations: CWRES, conditional weighted residuals; DV, dependent variable (observed); PK, pharmacokinetic; PRED, population predictions
L1691: Reviewers noticed that the Applicant tested several base structural models for efbemalenogastim, such as parallel baseline ANC-mediated nonlinear clearance and linear clearance, etc. However, all those models were ill-conditioned with condition number &gt;1000. This may be potentially due to imbalanced sample size and PK samples in lower dose levels (\le 120 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg}) versus higher dose levels (\ge 240 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg}). The estimation of PK parameters may be mainly driven by majority of PK data at doses \ge 240 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg}, where clearance of efbemalenogastim was probably saturated. Reviewers requested the Applicant to reconduct the PopPK analysis to reflect the nonlinear PK characteristics of efbemalenogastim at the studied dose range. However, the Applicant's updated models were still ill-conditioned with condition number &gt;1000. In addition, some covariate models failed to converge. Therefore, Reviewer conducted independent PopPK analysis using MONOLIX 2020 software in the following steps:
L1692: - Determine an optimal base structural model using PK data only from two dose escalation studies (GC-F-627-01 and 2012-F-627-CH1).
L1693: - Fix some PK parameters (e.g., maximum nonlinear clearance (V_{max}), efbemalenograstim concentration at half of V_m [K_m]), which was not able to be estimated accurately by pooling PK data at the saturated doses (i.e., \ge 240 \mu g/kg) from phase 3 studies.
- Incorporate time dependent clearance into base model to account for the remarkable difference in PK between cycle 1 and cycle 3 based on full PK dataset.
- Conduct covariate analysis using the full PK dataset to achieve the final PopPK model.
L1694: Based on the Reviewer's independent analysis, the PK of efbemalenograstim was best characterized by a one-compartment model with delayed first order absorption rate constant (t_{lag} and k_a), parallel linear (CL/F) and nonlinear elimination (V_{max}, K_m) together with time dependent clearance (TD_{max} and TD_{50}) on linear CL/F. The interindividual variability (IIV) was estimated as log-normally distributed with a nonzero covariance on apparent clearance (CL/F), apparent volume of distribution (V/F), with a block omega on CL/F and V/F.
L1695: Covariate analysis identified three statistically significant covariates, including effect of lean body weight (LBM) on V_{max}, effect of race (Chinese Han versus others) on V/F, and effect of age on K_a. Sex, weight, baseline ANC, BMI, formulation, and weight-adjusted dosing (0: no; 1: yes) had no clinically meaningful effect on efbemalenograstim PK. The equations of the final covariate models are the following:
L1696: k_a = 0.0096 \times \left(\frac{age}{47}\right)^{-0.42}
L1697: V_{max} = 66.3 \times \left(\frac{LBM}{47}\right)^{1.32}
L1698: V/F = 1.93 \times (1.15)^{race}
L1699: The final efbemalenograstim PK model parameter estimates and the corresponding 95% confidence intervals (CIs) from Bootstrap are presented in **Table 125**. PK parameters such as t_{lag}, k_a, V_{max}, and K_m were fixed to the estimated values from the base structure model based on intensive PK data from phase 1 studies.

L1716: ## Model Applications
L1717: - The final PopPK model was utilized to assess the clinically significant influence of the identified statistically significant covariates on the PK of efbemalenograstim. Simulations were performed with the final model in subjects on 20 mg dosage in cycle 1.
- Subjects' age ranged from 20 to 83 years in the clinical studies. The effect of age retained in the final PK model on AUC and C_{max} in cycle 1 are presented in Figure 42 and Figure 43, respectively. No clinically significant impact of age on efbemalenograstim efficacy and safety would be expected since AUC and C_{max} were largely overlapping between subjects \le 65 years and subjects &gt; 65 years.

L1722: (3) The body weight is not a significant covariate after incorporating the impact of LBM on V_{max} in the Reviewer's final PopPK model. Since there is a high correlation between body weight and LBM, the final PopPK model was also used to assess the influence of body weight on the PK of efbemalenograstim. The effect of body weight on AUC and C_{max} are presented in Figure 44 and Figure 45 respectively. There are no clinically significant differences in pharmacokinetics based on body weight.

L1726: **Figure 45. Influence of Body Weight on Efbemalenograstim C_{max} in the Reviewer's Final Model**
L1727: Source: Reviewer's analysis.
L1728: Abbreviations: C_{max}, maximum plasma concentration
L1729: Overall, Reviewers agree with the Applicant's the statement in Section 12.3 of labeling that no clinically significant differences were observed based on age (20 to 83 years) or body weight (43 to 137 kg).
L1730: - To assess the influence of immunogenicity on the PK of efbemalenograstim, the presence of antidrug antibody (ADA) was tested as a categorical variable in the final PopPK model.
L1731: The results showed that ADA was not a statistically significant covariate on any PK Parameters of efbemalenogastim. In addition, the final model was fit to the data excluding 30 subjects whose ADA test results were positive. The exclusion of these 30 subjects did not significantly change the final estimates and precision of PK parameters of final model. Taken together, PopPK analysis results suggested that the presence of ADA had no significant effect on the PK of efbemalenogastim, and thus no clinically meaningful impact of ADA on efbemalenogastim efficacy and safety is expected.
L1732: - The reviewer's final PopPK model was used to derive the individual PK parameters to support the labeling language in Section 12.3. The geometric mean (CV) of PK parameters in subjects with breast cancer who were on the recommended dosing regimen are presented in Table 126.
L1733: **Table 126. Summary of Individual Predicted Efbemalenogastim Exposure With Fixed Dose 20 mg, Cycle 1 and Cycle 3**

L1737: - The derived individual PK exposures from the reviewer's final model were further used for the following exposure-response (E-R) analyses for both efficacy and safety.
L1738: ## 14.5.2. Summary of Applicant's Exposure-Response Analysis

L1740: The Applicant's E-R for efficacy analysis of efbemalenogastim was conducted in 196 subjects from phase 3 trial GC-F-627-05. One subject from the efbemalenogastim treatment group was not included in this analysis due to lack of individual PK parameters. There was no missing efficacy data. The E-R analysis dataset covered a body weight range between 45 and 137 kg and an age range of 28 and 83 years old. L1747: The distribution of duration of severe neutropenia (DSN) in trial GC-627-05 is depicted in Figure 46. Approximately 88% of the subjects has zero day of DSN. Therefore, zero-inflated Poisson regression was selected to account for these zero observations.
L1748: Figure 46. Observed Frequency of DSN in Days, Trial GC-627-05
L1749: Source: gc-627-05-exposure-response-report figure 4-1.
L1750: Abbreviations: DSN, duration of severe neutropenia
L1751: The PK metric used by the Applicant for E-R analysis was efbemalenogastim AUC up to Day 21 in cycle 1 (AUC21d). Zero-inflated Poisson regression was conducted to quantitatively evaluate the relationship between efbemalenogastim AUC21d and DSN, while logistic regression analysis was conducted to quantify the correlation between efbemalenogastim AUC21d and incidence of severe neutropenia. Univariate analysis showed that there was no significant relationship between efbemalenogastim AUC21d and DSN (Figure 47) or incidence of severe neutropenia (Figure 48) within the relatively narrow exposure range at one dose of 20 mg.
L1752: **Figure 47. Observed and Model Predicted Correlation Between DSN and Efbemalenograstim AUC_{21d}**
L1753: Note: Open circles and bars represent means and 95% CI of observed DSN divided into four quartiles based on F-627 AUC_{21d}. Black lines and gray areas represent means and 95% confidence intervals of model predicted DSN. Univariate model with F-627 AUC_{21d} as the only covariate was used for plotting purpose.
L1754: Source: gc-627-05-exposure-response-report figure 4-3.

L1760: Multivariate analysis indicated that there was no effect of baseline body weight, country, baseline Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG), screening breast cancer stage, and prior chemotherapy on DSN. The parameter estimates from the multivariate zero-inflated Poisson
L1761: regression model that included efbemalenograstim exposure and age are listed in Table 129. The results indicated there was a positive correlation between age and DSN with a borderline p-value of 0.050; however, the effect of age was small with a slope of 0.037, i.e., 1 year of age increase would result in 0.037 days increase in DSN while other factors are constant. After including age in the model, efbemalenograstim exposure was still not a significant predictor for DSN.
L1762: **Table 129. Parameter Estimates of the Multivariate Zero-Inflated Poisson Regression Model**

L1766: **Reviewer's Comments:** Given that the Applicant's E-R analysis was based on individual PK exposures derived from an inappropriate PopPK model, Reviewer also performed an independent E-R analysis for efficacy with derived individual PK exposures (AUC21d, Cmax and Cmin) based on Reviewer's final PopPK model. Binary logistic regression analysis showed that there was no significant E-R relationship for incidence of severe neutropenia versus any of efbemalenograstim PK exposures. For example, as shown in Figure 49, there was a flat E-R relationship between efbemalenograstim logarithm transformed Cmin and the incidence of severe neutropenia.
L1767: **Figure 49. Exposure-Response Analysis for Incidence of Severe Neutropenia Using Binary Logistic Regression**
L1768: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L1769: Since the clinical efficacy endpoint, DSN is count data, both Poisson and Zero-inflated Poisson (ZIP) regression models were used for the E-R analysis. ZIP model showed a better prediction of observed DSN than the Poisson model. However, none of efbemalenogastim PK exposures was found a statistically significant predictor of DSN. As shown in the Figure 50 as an example, there was no significant E-R relationship between logarithm transformed AUC_{21d} and DSN.
L1770: **Figure 50. Exposure-Response Analysis for Duration of Severe Neutropenia Using Zero Inflated Poisson Model**
L1771: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L1772: Despite of different efbemalenogastim PK exposures used for E-R analysis, Reviewer's analysis results were consistent with the Applicant's E-R analysis results, which showed no significant E-R relationship for efficacy at the proposed dose of 20 mg. The flat E-R relationship for efficacy endpoints may imply that the effectiveness of efbemalenogastim reached the plateau, which seems to support the proposed dose (20 mg).
L1773: ## 14.5.2.2. Summary of Applicant's Exposure-Response for Safety
L1774: The dataset for the safety E-R analysis of efbemalenogastim comprised 196 subjects from trial GC-627-05. One subject from the efbemalenogastim treatment group was not included in this analysis due to lack of post hoc PK parameters. There was no missing safety endpoint data. The safety E-R analysis dataset covered a body weight range between 45 and 137 kg and an age range of 28 and 83 years old. Table 127 shows statistical summaries of baseline continuous covariates for the evaluable subjects, and Table 128 shows statistical summaries of baseline categorical covariates. The following safety endpoints were included in the E-R analysis, grade \ge 3 treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAE), grade \ge 2 alopecia, grade \ge 2 nausea, grade \ge 2 bone pain, grade \ge 2 asthenia, grade \ge 2 leukopenia, and grade \ge 2 thrombocytopenia. The Applicant concluded that no statistically significant E-R relationship was identified for these
L1775: safety measures at the exposure levels in trial GC-627-05 following the 20 mg fixed dose (Figure 51).

L1794: In summary, no statistically significant E-R relationships between efbemalenogastim PK exposures and efficacy endpoints, including DSN and incidence of severe neutropenia, or safety measures, including any TEAE, grade \ge 3 TEAE, grade \ge 2 alopecia, grade \ge 2 nausea, grade \ge 2 bone pain, grade \ge 2 asthenia, grade \ge 2 leukopenia, and grade \ge 2 thrombocytopenia at the 20 mg dose in trial GC-627-05. We also agree with the Applicant on potential limitations in the E-R analysis due to relatively narrow exposure range at a single dose level (20 mg) in trial GC-627-05. The absence of positive E-R relationships may suggest that the effectiveness of efbemalenogastim had already reached the plateau at the 20 mg fixed dose.

L1797: ## 15. Study/Trial Design
L1798: None.
L1799: ## 16. Efficacy
L1800: Results of additional secondary endpoints for Study GC-627-04 and Study GC-627-05 are described below.
L1801: In Study GC-627-04, secondary endpoints demonstrated additional benefits of F-627. The incidence of intravenous (IV) antibiotic use was 3.6% in subjects treated with F-627 and 17.9% in subjects treated with placebo in cycle 1. One subject was hospitalized for febrile neutropenia (FN) or infection in the placebo arm and no subjects were hospitalized for FN in the F-627 arm.
L1802: Secondary endpoints in Study GC-627-05 demonstrated that the efficacy of F-627 was no different from that of pegfilgrastim. The incidence of FN in the F-627 arm was 3% and 0% in the pegfilgrastim arm with an unadjusted treatment difference of 2.5% (95% confidence interval (CI): -7.3, 12.4). Intravenous antibiotics were used in 4.6% of subjects in the F-627 arm and in 1% of subjects in the pegfilgrastim arm for cycles 1-4 combined. One subject in each arm was hospitalized for FN or any infection during the study.
L1803: ## 17. Clinical Safety
L1804: ## Adverse Events of Special Interest (AEI)
L1805: Approximately one third of subjects (34.0%) experienced a TEAE identified as an adverse event of special interest (AEI) (reviewed in Section 7.2); few were serious (0.3%), and the highest proportion of subjects reported musculoskeletal pain. Events of musculoskeletal pain (e.g., back pain, bone pain, and headache) in the F-627 group were similar to pegfilgrastim.

L1821: Treatment-emergent adverse events defined as An AE if the date of onset was on or after the date of first chemotherapy treatment (in cycle 1) or was a worsening of a pre-existing medical condition (intensity and/or severity changed to a worsened grade). All AEs in subsequent cycles were considered TEAEs. AEs after the last cycle of treatment had been completed (i.e., Day 21 of the last cycle) were not considered treatment emergent.

L1829: ## 20. Mechanism of Action/Drug Resistance
L1830: Efbemalenogastim alfa-vuxw is a colony-stimulating factor that acts on hematopoietic cells by binding to specific cell surface receptors, thereby stimulating proliferation, differentiation, commitment, and end cell functional activation. See Section 5 for further details.

L1833: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L1834: ## Clinical Site Inspections
L1835: On-site clinical site inspections have not been conducted due to the SARS-Co-V2 pandemic. Instead the Food and Drug Administration (FDA) conducted a remote inspection.
L1836: Here is the language from their review:
L1837: *The FDA conducted a remote regulatory assessment (RRA) as an alternative to the onsite inspection. This was the first inspection of Evive Technology (Singapore) PTE. Ltd. This RRA covered Studies GC-627-04 and GC-627-05, respectively, submitted to the Agency in support of BLA 761134. Video conferencing via Zoom was conducted, and document sharing via an online platform (box.com) was deployed. This RRA was performed in accordance with Compliance Program 7348.810 Sponsors, Contract Research Organizations and Monitors and the assignment instructions.*
L1838: *Records from Sites 100 and 131 in Study GC-627-04 and from Sites 108 and 701 in Study GC-627-05 were evaluated. The following records were evaluated: organizational charts, standard operating procedures, investigator selection, monitoring plans, transfer of responsibilities, FDA 1572s, financial disclosure forms, subject protection and ethical oversite, safety plans, data management and investigational product accountability records.*
L1839: BLA 761134
Ryzneuta (efbemalenogastim alfa-vuxw)
L1840: *FDA also interviewed study personnel and reviewed regulatory files, source records, including subject selection, randomization, and blinding activities of four enrolled subjects, adverse events, IP accountability and efficacy endpoints.*
L1841: *Despite the limited scope due to the logistical constraints of information exchange between the FDA and Sponsor, the FDA assessment found that these sites were monitored throughout the studies, and clinical investigators carried out their responsibilities according to the FDA regulatory requirements.*
L1842: *In general, no significant deficiencies were observed during this remote regulatory assessment of the two studies in BLA 761134.*
L1843: ## 23. Labeling: Key Changes and Considerations

L1876: ## 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L1877: Pediatric assessments are required.
L1878: **PMR 1:** Conduct a multicenter, open-label, study to assess the safety, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of efbemalenogastim alfa in at least 40 pediatric patients 1 month to &lt;17 years of age with non-myeloid solid tumors or lymphomas receiving myelosuppressive anti-cancer drugs associated with a clinically significant incidence of febrile neutropenia.

L1881: Final Protocol Submission: 06/2025

L1884: **PMR 2:** Submit pediatric assessments for Ryzneuta (efbemalenogastim alfa) as described in section 505B(a)(2)(A) of the FD&amp;C Act, including development of an “appropriate formulation” (presentation) that can be used to directly and accurately administer Ryzneuta (efbemalenogastim alpha) to pediatric patients (1 month to &lt;17 years of age). Conduct any necessary human factors studies to evaluate the ability of healthcare providers and caregivers to measure the appropriate doses.

L1964: Figure 1A is a dose-response curve showing the bioactivity of F-627, rhG-CSF, and Pegfilgrastim on M-NSF-60 cells. The Y-axis represents Optical Density (OD) and the X-axis represents concentration in ng/mL (log scale). All three compounds show similar dose-response curves, indicating comparable potency in inducing proliferation in the M-NSF-60 cell line.
Dose-response curve showing Optical Density (OD) versus concentration (ng/mL) for F-627, rhG-CSF, and Pegfilgrastim on M-NSF-60 cells.
L1965: Bar chart showing the effect of F-627 (100 µg/kg) on Bone Marrow Activation (pSTAT3) relative to rhG-CSF (40 µg/kg) and Pegfilgrastim (40 µg/kg) in mice at 1 hr, 24 hr, 48 hr, and 72 hr postdose. The Y-axis is p-STAT3 (pg/mg) and the X-axis is Treatment (hrs). At 1 hr and 24 hr, F-627, rhG-CSF, and Pegfilgrastim show high p-STAT3 levels (around 130-150 pg/mg), while Base Line and Carrier show low levels (near 0). At 48 hr and 72 hr, all treatment groups show significantly reduced p-STAT3 levels, approaching Base Line and Carrier levels.
L1966: Figure 3 displays two graphs showing the changes in White Blood Cell (WBC) and Absolute Neutrophil Count (ANC) in 5-FU induced neutropenic mice treated with F-627 or Neulasta over 16 treatment days. The Y-axis is logarithmic.
The left graph shows WBC (\times 10^9/L) and the right graph shows ANC (\times 10^9/L). Both graphs compare the carrier control (dotted line) with F-627 doses of 30 \mu g/kg, 100 \mu g/kg, and 300 \mu g/kg, and Neulasta 100 \mu g/kg.
In the WBC graph, the carrier control shows a sharp decline to a nadir around Day 8 (approx. 1 \times 10^9/L) and then recovery. F-627 300 \mu g/kg shows the highest nadir (approx. 0.5 \times 10^9/L) and the fastest recovery, reaching levels above the carrier control by Day 12. F-627 100 \mu g/kg and 30 \mu g/kg show intermediate recovery. Neulasta 100 \mu g/kg shows a nadir around Day 8 (approx. 0.8 \times 10^9/L) and recovery.
In the ANC graph, the carrier control shows a nadir around Day 8 (approx. 0.5 \times 10^9/L). F-627 300 \mu g/kg shows the highest nadir (approx. 0.2 \times 10^9/L) and the fastest recovery, reaching levels above the carrier control by Day 12. F-627 100 \mu g/kg and 30 \mu g/kg show intermediate recovery. Neulasta 100 \mu g/kg shows a nadir around Day 8 (approx. 0.3 \times 10^9/L) and recovery.
L1967: Figure 4 displays two graphs showing the changes in White Blood Cell (WBC) and Neutrophil (NEUT) counts in Cyclophosphamide-induced neutropenic monkeys treated with F-627, rhG-CSF, or Neulasta over 19 study days (D1 to D19). The Y-axis is logarithmic.
The left graph shows WBC (10^9/L) and the right graph shows NEUT (10^9/L). Both graphs compare the CY group (Control), rhG-CSF 10 \mu g/kg, Neulasta 60 \mu g/kg, and F-627 doses of 25 \mu g/kg, 60 \mu g/kg, and 150 \mu g/kg.
In the WBC graph, the CY group shows a sharp decline to a nadir around Day 8 (approx. 1.0 10^9/L) and then recovery. F-627 60 \mu g/kg shows the highest nadir (approx. 0.5 10^9/L) and the fastest recovery, reaching levels above the CY group by Day 11. F-627 150 \mu g/kg and 25 \mu g/kg show intermediate recovery. rhG-CSF 10 \mu g/kg and Neulasta 60 \mu g/kg show recovery starting around Day 11.
In the NEUT graph, the CY group shows a nadir around Day 8 (approx. 0.1 10^9/L). F-627 60 \mu g/kg shows the highest nadir (approx. 0.05 10^9/L) and the fastest recovery, reaching levels above the CY group by Day 11. F-627 150 \mu g/kg and 25 \mu g/kg show intermediate recovery. rhG-CSF 10 \mu g/kg and Neulasta 60 \mu g/kg show recovery starting around Day 11.
L1968: Flowchart illustrating the Design Schema for Trial GC-627-04:
Stage II-IV Breast Cancer Patients (TA)
Cycle 1, Day 1: TA Chemotherapy, F-627 (20mg) or Placebo Randomization (2:1)
Branch 1 (N=80): Cycle 1, Day 2: F-627 20 mg SC. Followed by Cycles 2-4: TA Chemotherapy, F-627 20 mg SC (N=80).
Branch 2 (N=40): Cycle 1, Day 2: Placebo, SC. Followed by Cycles 2-4: TA Chemotherapy, F-627 20 mg SC (N=40).
L1969: Flowchart illustrating Missing ANC Data Imputation for Sensitivity Analysis:
Missing ANC Data branches into Intermittent and Monotone.
Intermittent branches into Rules for Assessment Window.
Rules for Assessment Window branches into F-627 MNAR and Others.
F-627 MNAR leads to MCMC - \delta.
Others leads to MCMC.
Monotone branches into F-627 MNAR Dropout and Others.
F-627 MNAR Dropout leads to Sequential Monotone Regression - \delta.
Others leads to Sequential Monotone Regression.
All imputation methods lead to the final step: Calculate Duration of Grade 4 Neutropenia.

Forest Plot Description: The plot displays the mean difference (Placebo - F-627) in days for the duration of grade 4 neutropenia across various sensitivity analyses. A vertical red line is drawn at 0.0, indicating the null difference. All confidence intervals (CIs) shown are above this line, suggesting that the mean difference is statistically significantly greater for placebo compared to F-627 across all analyses. The X-axis is labeled "Placebo - F-627 (Days)".
L1971: Line graph showing Mean (±SD) Absolute Neutrophil Count (ANC) over time (Cycle Day 1 to 22) for two treatment groups: F-627 20 mg (N=83, blue line) and Placebo (N=39, red line). The Y-axis is Neutrophils Count (x10^9/L) on a logarithmic scale (0.1 to 128). Both groups show a similar pattern of ANC decline starting around Day 3, reaching a nadir around Day 10 (Placebo ANC is lower than F-627 ANC at this point), and then recovering. By Day 15, both groups return to ANC levels similar to Day 1. Error bars represent standard deviation.

L2006: Box plot showing the influence of age on Efbemalenograstim C_{max} (ng/mL). The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 10 to 1E+04. The X-axis compares age groups: age \le 65 and age > 65. The age \le 65 group shows a higher median C_{max} (around 1000) compared to the age > 65 group (around 100). Both groups show similar interquartile ranges and overall distribution.
L2007: Scatter plot showing the influence of Body Weight (kg) on Efbemalenograstim AUC (ng*h/mL). The x-axis ranges from 40 to 140 kg. The y-axis ranges from 0e+00 to 3e+05 ng*h/mL. A fitted curve (blue line) and a shaded confidence interval (gray area) are shown. The AUC generally increases with body weight, reaching approximately 1.5e+05 ng*h/mL at 140 kg.

A fitted curve (blue line) and a shaded confidence interval (gray area) are shown. The Cmax generally increases with body weight, reaching approximately 1000 ng/mL at 140 kg.

L2012: Scatter plot showing the Probability of Incidence of Severe Neutropenia (%) versus Log(Cmin). The plot displays individual data points (circles) and a fitted logistic regression curve (solid line) with a shaded confidence interval. The x-axis (Log(Cmin)) ranges from -5 to 10. The y-axis (Probability of Incidence of Severe Neutropenia (%)) ranges from 0 to 100. The fitted curve is nearly flat, indicating no significant relationship between Log(Cmin) and the incidence of severe neutropenia.
L2013: Scatter plot showing DSN (days) on the Y-axis (0 to 5) versus Log(AUC_21d) on the X-axis (0 to 8). The data points are scattered, and a fitted curve (likely a zero-inflated Poisson model) is shown, indicating a flat relationship between Log(AUC_21d) and DSN, suggesting no significant exposure-response relationship.

The red solid line represents the logistic regression line, and the shaded area represents the 95% confidence interval. Black boxes with error bars represent the observed proportion within each exposure quartile.
The six AEs analyzed are Alopecia, Astenia, Bone pain, Grade \ge 3 TEAE, Leukopenia, and Nausea.
